Continuing work from thread T-019b36b4-071f-715c-9c40-e838c331c5a3. When you lack specific information you can use read_thread to get it.

@docs/BENCHMARKING.md @docs/EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md @docs/ROADMAP.md @docs/CHANGELOG.md @Cargo.toml

- I just added comprehensive tests and benchmarks for file system watching and workspace features
- I created a new benchmarking guide at docs/BENCHMARKING.md that documents all benchmark suites, profiling techniques, and performance targets
- The codebase uses divan crate for benchmarking, not criterion
- Profiling profile is defined in Cargo.toml with [profile.profiling] inherits = "release" with debug = true
- There's an optional dhat-heap feature for heap profiling
- Existing benchmark suites: rope_operations, rendering, main_loop, syntax, layout, search, glyph_cache, workspace (new)

- I need to review documentation files for accuracy against the actual codebase
- Key areas to verify: theme system docs, keymap docs, and any restructured docs
- Should check git unstaged changes to see what documentation was modified or added
- The editor uses Elm-style architecture with messages in src/messages.rs, update handlers in src/update/
- Theme system is in src/theme/
- Keymap system is in src/keymap/

lets look into the newly added docs and restructured documentation files (check git unstaged), lets review each of them for accuracy with the actual codebase (especially the theme and keymap docs),

themes**: Embedded at compile time from `themes/` directory\n25: - **User themes**: `~/.config/token/themes/` (create directory to add custom themes)\n26: \n27: ### Switching Themes\n28: \n29: Use the Command Palette (Cmd+Shift+A) and search for \"Switch Theme\" to change themes.\n30: \n31: ---\n32: \n33: ## Theme Format\n34: \n35: Themes use YAML with a structured format:\n36: \n37: ```yaml\n38: version: 1\n39: name: \"My Custom Theme\"\n40: author: \"Your Name\"\n41: description: \"A brief description of the theme\"\n42: \n43: ui:\n44:   editor:\n45:     background: \"#1E1E1E\"\n46:     foreground: \"#D4D4D4\"\n47:     # ... more colors\n48: \n49:   syntax:\n50:     keyword: \"#C586C0\"\n51:     function: \"#DCDCAA\"\n52:     # ... more colors\n53: ```\n54: \n55: ### Color Format\n56: \n57: Colors use hexadecimal format:\n58: - **6-digit**: `#RRGGBB` (e.g., `#1E1E1E`)\n59: - **8-digit**: `#RRGGBBAA` with alpha channel (e.g., `#FFFFFF80` for 50% opacity)\n60: \n61: 1 - Navigation | [command-palette-enhancements.md](feature/command-palette-enhancements.md) |\n69: | Quick Open | 1 - Navigation | [quick-open.md](feature/quick-open.md) |\n70: | Go to Line |\n77: | Auto-Save | 3 - File Lifecycle | [auto-save.md](feature/auto-save.md) |\n78: | File Change Detection | 3 - File Lifecycle | [file-change-detection.md](feature/file-change-detection.md) |\n79: Soft Wrap | 4 - Hard Problems | [soft-wrap.md](feature/soft-wrap.md) |\n82: | Code Folding | 4 - Hard Problems | [code-folding.md](feature/code-folding.md) |\n83: | Diff Gutter | 5 - Insight Tools | [diff-gutter.md](feature/diff-gutter.md) |\n84: | Markdown Preview | 5 - Insight Tools | [markdown-preview.md](feature/markdown-preview.md) |\n85: | Snippets | 6 - Productivity | [snippets.md](feature/snippets.md) |\n86: \n87: ---\n88: \n89: ## Architecture\n90: \n91: Token follows the **Elm Architecture** pattern:\n92: \n93: ```\n94: User Input → Message → Update → Command → Render\n95:      ↑                                      │\n96:      └──────────────────────────────────────┘\n97: ```\n98: \n99: ### Core Modules\n100: \n101: | Module | Location | Purpose |\n102: |--------|----------|---------|\n103: | Model | `src/model/` | AppModel, Document, EditorState |\n104: | Messages | `src/messages.rs` | Msg, EditorMsg, DocumentMsg |\n105: | Update | `src/update/` | Pure state transformation |\n106: | View | `src/view/` | CPU rendering pipeline |\n107: | Keymap | `src/keymap/` | Configurable keybindings |\n108: \n109: ### Module Map\n110: \n111: ```\n112: src/\n113: ├── main.rs              # Entry point\n114: ├── lib.rs               # Library root\n115: ├── model/               # State structures\n116: │   ├── document.rs      # Text buffer, undo/redo\n117: │   ├── editor.rs        # Cursor, Selection, Viewport\n118: │   ├── editor_area.rs   # Splits, tabs, groups\n119: │   └── workspace.rs     # File tree\n120: ├── update/              # State transformation\n121: │   ├── editor.rs        # Cursor movement, selection\n122: │   ├── document.rs      # Text editing\n123: │   └── layout.rs        # Splits, tabs\n124: ├── view/                # Rendering\n125: │   ├── mod.rs           # Renderer\n126: │   └── frame.rs         # Drawing primitives\n127: ├── keymap/              # Key handling\n128: │   ├── keymap.rs        # Lookup engine\n129: │   └── defaults.rs      # Default bindings\n130: └── syntax/              # Highlighting\n131:     └── worker.rs        # Background parser\n132: ```\n133: \n134: ---\n135: \n136: ## Contributing\n137: \n138: ### Before You Start\n139: \n140: 1. Check [ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md) for planned work\n141: 2. Read the relevant feature design doc in `docs/feature/`\n142: 3. Understand the behavior contracts in `docs/dev/`\n143: \n144: ### Creating a Feature Doc\n145: \n146: 1. Copy [templates/FEATURE_SPEC.md](templates/FEATURE_SPEC.md)\n147: 2. Fill in all sections\n148: 3. Submit for review before implementation\n149: \n150: ### Definition of Done\n151: \n152: For each feature:\n153: \n154: - [ ] Commands appear in palette + documented\n155: - [ ] Default keybinding + configurable override\n156: - [ ] Tests cover 3+ edge cases\n157: - [ ] No regression to selection/cursor invariants\n158: - [ ] Screenshot/GIF for website (if user-visible)\n159: \n160: ---\n161: \n162: ## References\n163: \n164: - [ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md) - Feature roadmap and milestones\n165: - [CHANGELOG.md](CHANGELOG.md) - Version history\n166: - [EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md](EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md) - UI component reference\n167: - [BENCHMARKING.md](BENCHMARKING.md) - Performance testing guide",

{
  "content": "1: # Contributing to Token\n2: \n3: Thank you for your interest in contributing to Token!\n4: \n5: ---\n6: \n7: ## Development Setup\n8: \n9: 1. **Install Rust**: Get the [Rust toolchain](https://rustup.rs/)\n10: \n11: 2. **Clone and build**:\n12:    ```bash\n13:    git clone https://github.com/HelgeSverre/token\n14:    cd token\n15:    make setup    # Install dev dependencies\n16:    make build    # Build debug binary\n17:    ```\n18: \n19: 3. **Run**:\n20:    ```bash\n21:    make dev      # Run debug build\n22:    make run      # Run release build\n23:    ```\n24: \n25: ---\n26: \n27: ## Running Tests\n28: \n29: ```bash\n30: make test           # Run all tests\n31: make test-verbose   # Run with output\n32: make test-fast      # Fast parallel tests (nextest)\n33: ```\n34: \n35: ---\n36: \n37: ## Code Style\n38: \n39: - **Format before committing**: Run `make fmt`\n40: - **Lint check**: Run `make lint` (mirrors CI)\n41: - **Follow existing patterns**: Match the style of surrounding code\n42: \n43: ---\n44: \n45: ## Smart Commits\n46: \n47: This project includes a `/commit` slash command for Claude Code that:\n48: - Analyzes staged and unstaged changes\n49: - Creates atomic commits following Conventional Commits format\n50: - Groups related changes logically\n51: \n52: Usage in Claude Code:\n53: ```\n54: /commit\n55: /commit refactoring the authentication flow\n56: ```\n57: \n58: ---\n59: \n60: ## Pull Requests\n61: \n62: - **One feature/fix per PR**: Keep changes focused\n63: - **Include tests**: Add tests for new functionality\n64: - **Update docs**: If your change affects user-facing behavior, update relevant docs\n65: - **Descriptive commits**: Use clear, concise commit messages\n66: \n67: ---\n68: \n69: ## Architecture Overview\n70: \n71: Token follows the **Elm Architecture** pattern:\n72: \n73: ```\n74: Message → Update → Command → Render\n75: ```\n76: \n77: Key files:\n78: - `src/model/` - Data structures (AppModel, Document, EditorState)\n79: - `src/update/` - Pure state transformation\n80: - `src/view.rs` - Rendering\n81: - `src/input.rs` - Keyboard input handling\n82: \n83: For detailed architecture documentation, see:\n84: - [EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md](EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md) - Viewport, coordinates, scrolling\n85: - [ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md) - Planned features\n86: - [CHANGELOG.md](CHANGELOG.md) - Version history\n87: \n88: ---\n89: \n90: ## Getting Help\n91: \n92: - Open an issue for bugs or feature requests\n93: - Check existing issues before creating new ones",
  "absolutePath": "~/code/rust-editor/docs/CONTRIBUTING.md"
}

Arrows: up, down, left, right\n14: #   - Navigation: home, end, pageup, pagedown, insert\n15: #   - Function keys: f1, f2, ... f12\n16: #   - Numpad: numpad0-9, numpad_add, numpad_subtract, etc.\n17: #\n18: # Format:\n19: #   - key: \"modifier+key\" or just \"key\"\n20: #     command: CommandName\n21: #\n22: # Use \"cmd\" for the platform command key (Cmd on macOS, Ctrl elsewhere)\n23: \n24: bindings:\n25:   # ===========================================================================\n26:   # File Operations\n27:   # ===========================================================================\n28:   - key: \"cmd+s\"\n29:     command: SaveFile\n30: \n31:   - key: \"cmd+shift+s\"\n32:     command: SaveFileAs\n33: \n34:   - key: \"cmd+o\"\n35:     command: OpenFile\n36: \n37:   - key: \"cmd+shift+o\"\n38:     command: OpenFolder\n39: \n40:   - {
  "content": "1: //! Command enum representing all executable editor actions\n2: //!\n3: //! Commands are the bridge between keybindings and the message system.\n4: //! Each command maps to one or more `Msg` values for the Elm-style update loop.\n5: \n6: use crate::messages::{\n7:     AppMsg, CsvMsg, Direction, DocumentMsg, EditorMsg, LayoutMsg, Msg, UiMsg, WorkspaceMsg,\n8: };\n9: use crate::model::editor_area::SplitDirection;\n10: use crate::model::ModalId;\n11: \n12: /// All executable editor commands that can be bound to keys\n13: ///\n14: /// This enum covers every action that can be triggered via keyboard.\n15: /// Commands are organized by category for clarity.\n16: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]\n17: pub enum Command {\n18:     // ========================================================================\n19:     // Cursor Movement (no selection)\n20:     // ========================================================================\n21:     /// Move cursor up one line\n22:     MoveCursorUp,\n23:     /// Move cursor down one line\n24:     MoveCursorDown,\n25:     /// Move cursor left one character\n26:     MoveCursorLeft,\n27:     /// Move cursor right one character\n28:     MoveCursorRight,\n29:     /// Move cursor to start of line\n30:     MoveCursorLineStart,\n31:     /// Move cursor to end of line\n32:     MoveCursorLineEnd,\n33:     /// Move cursor to start of document\n34:     MoveCursorDocumentStart,\n35:     /// Move cursor to end of document\n36:     MoveCursorDocumentEnd,\n37:     /// Move cursor left by word\n38:     MoveCursorWordLeft,\n39:     /// Move cursor right by word\n40:     MoveCursorWordRight,\n41:     /// Move cursor up by page\n42:     PageUp,\n43:     /// Move cursor down by page\n44:     PageDown,\n45: \n46:     // ========================================================================\n47:     // Selection Movement (extend selection)\n48:     // ========================================================================\n49:     /// Move cursor up, extending selection\n50:     MoveCursorUpWithSelection,\n51:     /// Move cursor down, extending selection\n52:     MoveCursorDownWithSelection,\n53:     /// Move cursor left, extending selection\n54:     MoveCursorLeftWithSelection,\n55:     /// Move cursor right, extending selection\n56:     MoveCursorRightWithSelection,\n57:     /// Move to line start, extending selection\n58:     MoveCursorLineStartWithSelection,\n59:     /// Move to line end, extending selection\n60:     MoveCursorLineEndWithSelection,\n61:     /// Move to document start, extending selection\n62:     MoveCursorDocumentStartWithSelection,\n63:     /// Move to document end, extending selection\n64:     MoveCursorDocumentEndWithSelection,\n65:     /// Move left by word, extending selection\n66:     MoveCursorWordLeftWithSelection,\n67:     /// Move right by word, extending selection\n68:     MoveCursorWordRightWithSelection,\n69:     /// Page up, extending selection\n70:     PageUpWithSelection,\n71:     /// Page down, extending selection\n72:     PageDownWithSelection,\n73: \n74:     // ========================================================================\n75:     // Selection Commands\n76:     // ========================================================================\n77:     /// Select all text in document\n78:     SelectAll,\n79:     /// Select current word\n80:     SelectWord,\n81:     /// Select current line\n82:     SelectLine,\n83:     /// Clear selection (collapse to cursor)\n84:     ClearSelection,\n85:     /// Expand selection to larger semantic unit\n86:     ExpandSelection,\n87:     /// Shrink selection to smaller semantic unit\n88:     ShrinkSelection,\n89: \n90:     // ========================================================================\n91:     // Multi-Cursor\n92:     // ========================================================================\n93:     /// Add a cursor above the current cursor\n94:     AddCursorAbove,\n95:     /// Add a cursor below the current cursor\n96:     AddCursorBelow,\n97:     /// Collapse to single cursor (remove all secondary cursors)\n98:     CollapseToSingleCursor,\n99:     /// Select next occurrence of current word/selection\n100:     SelectNextOccurrence,\n101:     /// Unselect the last added occurrence\n102:     UnselectOccurrence,\n103: \n104:     // ========================================================================\n105:     // Text Editing\n106:     // ========================================================================\n107:     /// Insert a newline at cursor\n108:     InsertNewline,\n109:     /// Delete character before cursor (backspace)\n110:     DeleteBackward,\n111:     /// Delete character at cursor (delete)\n112:     DeleteForward,\n113:     /// Delete word before cursor\n114:     DeleteWordBackward,\n115:     /// Delete word after cursor\n116:     DeleteWordForward,\n117:     /// Delete entire line\n118:     DeleteLine,\n119:     /// Duplicate current line or selection\n120:     Duplicate,\n121:     /// Indent selected lines\n122:     IndentLines,\n123:     /// Unindent selected lines\n124:     UnindentLines,\n125:     /// Insert a tab character (when no selection)\n126:     InsertTab,\n127: \n128:     // ========================================================================\n129:     // Clipboard\n130:     // ========================================================================\n131:     /// Copy selection to clipboard\n132:     Copy,\n133:     /// Cut selection to clipboard\n134:     Cut,\n135:     /// Paste from clipboard\n136:     Paste,\n137: \n138:     // ========================================================================\n139:     // Undo/Redo\n140:     // ========================================================================\n141:     /// Undo last change\n142:     Undo,\n143:     /// Redo last undone change\n144:     Redo,\n145: \n146:     // ========================================================================\n147:     // File Operations\n148:     // ========================================================================\n149:     /// Save current file\n150:     SaveFile,\n151:     /// Save current file with new name\n152:     SaveFileAs,\n153:     /// Open file dialog\n154:     OpenFile,\n155:     /// Open folder dialog\n156:     OpenFolder,\n157:     /// Create new file\n158:     NewFile,\n159:     /// Quit application\n160:     Quit,\n161: \n162:     // ========================================================================\n163:     // Modals/Dialogs\n164:     // ToggleGotoLine,\n169:     /// Toggle find/replace dialog\n170:     ToggleFindReplace,\n171: \n172:     // ========================================================================\n173:     // Layout (Tabs/Splits)\n174:     // ========================================================================\n175:     /// Create new tab\n176:     NewTab,\n177:     /// Close current tab\n178:   "content": "1: //! Theme system for the editor\n2: //!\n3: //! Provides YAML-based theming support with compile-time embedded themes\n4: //! and user-defined themes from config directories.\n5: //!\n6: //! Theme loading priority:\n7: //! 1. User config: `~/.config/token-editor/themes/{id}.yaml`\n8: //! 2. Embedded: Built-in themes compiled into binary\n9: \n10: use std::path::Path;\n11: \n12: use serde::Deserialize;\n13: \n14: // Embed theme YAML files at compile time\n15: pub const DEFAULT_DARK_YAML: &str = include_str!(\"../themes/dark.yaml\");\n16: pub const FLEET_DARK_YAML: &str = include_str!(\"../themes/fleet-dark.yaml\");\n17: pub const GITHUB_DARK_YAML: &str = include_str!(\"../themes/github-dark.yaml\");\n18: pub const GITHUB_LIGHT_YAML: &str = include_str!(\"../themes/github-light.yaml\");\n19: \n20: /// A built-in theme entry\n21: pub struct BuiltinTheme {\n22:     /// Stable identifier for config (e.g. \"default-dark\", \"fleet-dark\")\n23:     pub id: &'static str,\n24:     /// Embedded YAML content\n25:     pub yaml: &'static str,\n26: }\n27: \n28: /// Registry of all built-in themes\n29: pub const BUILTIN_THEMES: &[BuiltinTheme] = &[\n30:     BuiltinTheme {\n31:         id: \"default-dark\",\n32:         yaml: DEFAULT_DARK_YAML,\n33:     },\n34:     BuiltinTheme {\n35:         id: \"fleet-dark\",\n36:         yaml: FLEET_DARK_YAML,\n37:     },\n38:     BuiltinTheme {\n39:         id: \"github-dark\",\n40:         yaml: [References](#references)\n22: \n23: ---\n24: \n25: ## Overview\n26: \n27: ### Current State\n28: \n29: The editor currently:\n30: - Uses horizontal scrolling for long lines\n31: - Renders one logical line = one visual line\n32: - Cursor positions are `(line, column)` in logical coordinates\n33: - Viewport tracks `top_line` and `left_column` for scrolling\n34: \n35: ### Goals\n36: \n37: 1. **Per-pane soft wrap toggle**: Each editor view can independently enable/disable wrapping\n38: 2. **Logical/visual line mapping**: Cursor operations work on logical lines, display uses visual lines\n39: 3. **Wrap at word boundaries**: Prefer breaking at whitespace\n40: 4. **Preserve cursor position**: Toggle wrap without jumping cursor\n41: 5. **Correct scrolling**: Viewport calculates visible area based on visual lines\n42: 6. **Gutter line numbers**: Show logical line numbers, with continuation indicator for wrapped lines\n43: 7. **Selection across wraps**: Selections that span wrapped lines render correctly\n44: \n45: ### Non-Goals\n46: \n47: - Hard wrap (reformatting document)\n48: - Wrap width configuration (use viewport width)\n49: - Wrap at character level only (always prefer word boundaries)\n50: - Bidirectional text support\n[... omitted lines 51 to 839 ...]",

{
  "content": "1: # AI-Assisted Development: Building Token\n2: \n3: Token is a multi-cursor text editor written in Rust—around 15,000 lines of code built primarily through 116 conversations with [Amp Code](https://ampcode.com/@helgesverre).\n4: \n5: This document describes the methodology that made sustained AI collaboration work on a project that doesn't fit in a single context window. It's not about what AI can do in a demo; it's about what actually works across months of development.\n6: \n7: The core insight: **AI agents excel at focused, well-defined tasks. The human's job is to provide structure—clear phases, written specifications, and explicit invariants.** The more complex your project, the more this structure pays off.\n8: \n9: Text editors are a good stress test for this approach. They look simple but hide real complexity: cursor choreography with selection, keyboard modifier edge cases across platforms, grapheme cluster handling, viewport scrolling that \"feels right.\" Getting these details consistent across dozens of AI sessions requires explicit documentation that both humans and machines can reference. See [EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md](EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md) for what that looks like in practice.\n10: \n11: ---\n12: \n13: ## The Framework\n14: \n15: ### Three Modes of Work\n16: \n17: Before each session, explicitly state which mode you're in:\n18: \n19: | Mode        | Purpose                                       | Inputs                       | Example                          |\n20: | ----------- | --------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------- | -------------------------------- |\n21: | **Build**   | New behavior that didn't exist                | Feature spec, reference docs | \"Implement split view (Phase 3)\" |\n22: | **Improve** | Better architecture without changing behavior | Organization docs, roadmap   | \"Extract modules from main.rs\"   |\n23: | **Sweep**   | Fix a cluster of related bugs                 | Bug tracker, gap doc         | \"Multi-cursor selection bugs\"    |\n24: \n25: This prevents scope creep and keeps AI contributions coherent across sessions.\n26: \n27: ---\n28: \n29: ### Design Before Code\n30: \n31: For complex features, invest in upfront documentation:\n32: \n33: **1. Reference Documentation**\n34: Create a \"document of truth\" for cross-cutting concerns. [EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md](EDITOR_UI_REFERENCE.md) defines viewport math, coordinate systems, and scrolling behavior—used across split view, selection, and overlay implementations.\n35: \n36: **2. Feature Specifications**\n37: Before implementing multi-cursor, we wrote [SELECTION_MULTICURSOR.md](archived/SELECTION_MULTICURSOR.md):\n38: \n39: - Data structures and invariants\n40: - Keyboard shortcuts table\n41: - Message enums and expected behavior\n42: - Multi-phase implementation plan\n43: \n44: **3. Gap Documents**\n45: When a feature is 60-90% complete, create a gap doc. [MULTI_CURSOR_SELECTION_GAPS.md](archived/MULTI_CURSOR_SELECTION_GAPS.md) lists:\n46: \n47: - What's implemented vs. missing\n48: - Design decisions for each gap\n49: - Tests and success criteria\n50: \n51: This turns \"vague incompleteness\" into concrete, actionable tasks.\n52: \n53: ---\n54: \n55: ### The Multi-Cursor Migration: A Case Study\n56: \n57: Adding multi-cursor to a single-cursor editor touched nearly every file. All done. Here's a summary of the changes:

### Summary

#### Documentation Fixes
1. **[docs/KEYBINDINGS.md](file://~/code/rust-editor/docs/KEYBINDINGS.md)** - Fixed config path from `~/.config/token/` to `~/.config/token-editor/`
2. **[docs/THEMES.md](file://~/code/rust-editor/docs/THEMES.md)** - Fixed config path + added CSV theme documentation
3. **[docs/CONTRIBUTING.md](file://~/code/rust-editor/docs/CONTRIBUTING.md)** - Updated outdated module paths

#### CSV Theme Support
1. **[src/theme.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/theme.rs#L239-L241)** - Added `csv: CsvThemeData` field to `UiThemeData` for YAML parsing
2. **[src/theme.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/theme.rs#L757-L805)** - Refactored `from_data()` to build editor/gutter first, then pass to `CsvTheme::from_data()`
3. **Theme files** - Added explicit `csv:` sections to all 4 built-in themes:
   - [themes/dark.yaml](file://~/code/rust-editor/themes/dark.yaml#L54-L60)
   - [themes/fleet-dark.yaml](file://~/code/rust-editor/themes/fleet-dark.yaml#L54-L60)
   - [themes/github-dark.yaml](file://~/code/rust-editor/themes/github-dark.yaml#L54-L60)
   - [themes/github-light.yaml](file://~/code/rust-editor/themes/github-light.yaml#L54-L60)

CSV themes are now fully customizable via YAML, with intelligent fallbacks to gutter/editor colors when not specified.

This improves navigation in large files and helps focus on relevant code sections.\n17: \n18: ---\n19: \n20: ## Features\n21: \n22: ### Core Capabilities\n23: \n24: | Feature | Description |\n25: |---------|-------------|\n26: | Fold/Unfold | Collapse/expand individual regions |\n27: | Fold All | Collapse all foldable regions |\n28: | Unfold All | Expand all folded regions |\n29: | Fold Level N | Fold to specific nesting depth |\n30: | Fold Selection | Create custom fold from selection |\n31: | Persistent Folds | Remember fold state across sessions |\n32: \n33: ### Fold Sources\n34: \n35: | Source | Description | Priority |\n36: |--------|-------------|----------|\n37: | Syntax-based | Language constructs (functions, classes) | P1 |\n38: | Indentation-based | Indent levels (Python, YAML) | P1 |\n39: | Region markers | `// #region` / `// #endregion` | P2 |\n40: | Manual | User-created folds | P3 |\n41: \n42: ---\n43: \n44: ## [Overview](#overview)\n16: 2. [Architecture](#architecture)\n17: 3. [Data Structures](#data-structures)\n18: 4. [Keybindings](#keybindings)\n19: 5. [Implementation Plan](#implementation-plan)\n20: 6. [References](#references)\n22: \n23: ---\n24: \n25: ## Overview\n26: \n27: ### Current State\n28: \n29: The editor currently:\n30: - Uses horizontal scrolling for long lines\n31: - Renders one logical line = one visual line\n32: - Cursor positions are `(line, column)` in logical coordinates\n33: - Viewport tracks `top_line` and `left_column` for scrolling\n34: \n35: ### Goals\n36: \n37: 1. **Per-pane soft wrap toggle**: Each editor view can independently enable/disable wrapping\n38: 2. **Logical/visual line mapping**: Cursor operations work on logical lines, display uses visual lines\n39: 3. **Wrap at word boundaries**: Prefer breaking at whitespace\n40: 4. **Preserve cursor position**: Toggle wrap without jumping cursor\n41: 5. **Correct scrolling**: Viewport calculates visible area based on visual lines\n42: 6. **Gutter line numbers**: Show logical line numbers, with continuation indicator for wrapped lines\n43: 7. **Selection across wraps**: Selections that span wrapped lines render correctly\n44: \n45: ### Non-Goals\n46: \n47: - Hard wrap (reformatting document)\n48: - Wrap width configuration (use viewport width)\n49: - Wrap at character level only (always prefer word boundaries)\n50: - Bidirectional text support\n51: \n52: ---\n53: \n54: ## Architecture\n55: \n56: ```\n57: └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │\n66: │                                                                              │\n67: │  Visual Lines (with wrap width = 30):                                        │\n68: │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │\n69: │  │ VLine 0: \"This is a very long line \"      ← Logical Line 0, part 1  │   │\n70: │  │ VLine 1: \"that needs to wrap around...\"   ← Logical Line 0, part 2  │   │\n71: │  │ VLine 2: \"Short line\"                     ← Logical Line 1          │   │\n72: │  │ VLine 3: \"Another long line that \"        ← Logical Line 2, part 1  │   │\n73: │  │ VLine 4: \"will require wrapping at \"      ← Logical Line 2, part 2  │   │\n74: │  │ VLine 5: \"the...\" [References](#references)\n22: \n23: ---\n24: \n25: ## Overview\n26: \n27: ### Current State\n28: \n29: The editor renders text with basic whitespace handling:\n30: - Tabs expanded to spaces for display\n31: - No visual indicators for whitespace characters\n32: - No indent guides\n33: - No trailing whitespace highlighting\n34: \n35: ### Goals\n36: \n37: 1. **Indent guides** - Vertical lines showing indentation levels\n38: 2. **Tab markers** - Visual indicator for tab characters\n39: 3. **Space markers** - Optional dots for space characters\n40: 4. **EOL markers** - Optional newline/CR/CRLF indicators\n41: 5. **Trailing whitespace** - Highlight trailing spaces and tabs\n42: 6. **Configurable** - User can toggle each feature independently\n43: 7. **Theme support** - Colors from theme definition\n44: \n45: ### Non-Goals\n46: \n47: - Word wrap guides (separate feature)\n48: - Bracket pair guides (different from indent guides)\n49: - Minimap whitespace (minimap is separate feature)\n50: \n51: ---\n52: \n53: ## Architecture\n54: \n55: ```\n56: ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n57: │                      Whitespace Rendering System                             │\n58: ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤\n59: │                                                                              │\n60: │  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │\n61: │  │                          Visual Examples                                │ │\n62: │  │                                                                         │ │\n63: │  │  Indent Guides (vertical bars at indentation levels):                  │ │\n64: │  │                                                                         │ │\n65: │  │  fn main() {                                                           │ │\n66: │  │  │   let x = 5;   "content": "1: # Whitespace Management (F-090)\n2: \n3: Tools for visualizing, converting, and cleaning up whitespace in documents.\n4: \n5: > **Status:** 📋 Planned\n6: > **Priority:** P3 (Nice-to-have)\n7: > **Effort:** M (3-5 days)\n8: > **Created:** 2025-12-19\n9: > **Updated:** 2025-12-19\n10: > **Milestone:** 2 - Search & Editing\n11: \n12: ---\n13: \n14: ## Overview\n15: \n16: Whitespace management provides tools to visualize invisible characters, convert between tabs and spaces, and trim unnecessary whitespace. These features help maintain consistent code formatting and catch whitespace-related issues.\n17: \n18: ---\n19: \n20: ## Features\n21: \n22: ### 1. Whitespace Visualization\n23: \n24: Show invisible characters (spaces, tabs, newlines) with visible markers.\n25: \n26: #### Display Modes\n27: \n28: | Mode | Description |\n29: |------|-------------|\n30: | None | No whitespace visualization (default) |\n31: | Selection | Show whitespace only in selected text |\n32: | Trailing | Show only trailing whitespace |\n33: | All | Show all whitespace characters |\n34: \n35: #### Visual Markers\n36: \n37: | Character | Symbol | Description |\n38: |-----------|--------|-------------|\n39: | Space | `·` (U+00B7) | Middle dot |\n40: | Tab | `→` (U+2192) | Right arrow |\n41: | Newline | `↵` (U+21B5) | Carriage return symbol |\n42: | NBSP | `°` (U+00B0) | Degree symbol |\n43: \n44: #### Theme Integration\n45: \n46: ```yaml\n47: # themes/dark.yaml\n48: whitespace:\n49:   marker: \"#4a4a4a\"        # Subdued color for markers\n50:   trailing_bg: \"#3a2020\"   # Background for trailing whitespace\n51: ```\n52: \n53: ### 2. Tab/Space Conversion\n54: \n55: Convert between tabs and spaces while preserving alignment.\n56: \n57: #### Commands\n58: \n59: | Command | Description |\n60: |---------|-------------|\n61: | Convert Indentation to Spaces | Replace leading tabs with spaces |\n62: | Convert Indentation to Tabs | Replace leading spaces with tabs |\n63: | Convert All Tabs to Spaces | Replace all tabs (not just leading) |\n64: | Convert All Spaces to Tabs | Replace all space runs with tabs |\n65: \n66: #### Algorithm: Tabs to Spaces\n67: \n68: ```rust\n69: fn convert_tabs_to_spaces(doc: &mut Document, tab_size: usize) {\n70:     let text = doc.buffer.to_string();\n71:     let mut result = String::new();\n72:     let mut column = 0;\n73: \n74:     for ch in text.chars() {\n75:         match ch {\n76:             '\\t' => {\n77:                 // Calculate spaces to next tab stop\n78:                 let spaces = tab_size - (column % tab_size);\n79:                 result.push_str(&\" \".repeat(spaces));\n80:                 column += spaces;\n[... omitted lines 81 to 441 ...]",

}

### Documentation Inaccuracy Report

After reviewing the new feature docs against the actual codebase, here are the issues found:

#### 🔴 Critical Inaccuracies (Incorrect Code References)

1. **[select-next-occurrence.md](file://~/code/rust-editor/docs/feature/select-next-occurrence.md#L43)**
   - **Claims:** `Command::SelectAllOccurrences` exists in `src/keymap/command.rs`
   - **Reality:** `SelectAllOccurrences` exists only as `EditorMsg` in messages.rs, NOT as a `Command` in command.rs. There is no keybinding for it.
   - **Fix:** Remove the line `Command::SelectAllOccurrences,` from the code block or note it's not wired to a command yet

2. **[line-operations.md](file://~/code/rust-editor/docs/feature/line-operations.md#L32-L33)**
   - **Claims:** `Command::MoveLineUp` and `Command::MoveLineDown` exist
   - **Reality:** These commands do NOT exist in command.rs
   - **Fix:** Change "Exists" comments to "Does not exist - needs to be added"

#### 🟡 Minor Inaccuracies (Version References)

3. **[diff-gutter.md](file://~/code/rust-editor/docs/feature/diff-gutter.md#L35)** and **[file-change-detection.md](file://~/code/rust-editor/docs/feature/file-change-detection.md#L30)**
   - **Claims:** File system watcher was added in "v0.3.10"
   - **Reality:** Current version is v0.3.9. The fs_watcher module exists but was never documented in changelog and there is no v0.3.10 yet.
   - **Fix:** Change "v0.3.10" to "v0.3.9" or remove the version reference

#### ✅ Verified Accurate

All other references checked were accurate:
- `OccurrenceState` in editor.rs ✓
- `CommandPaletteState` in ui.rs ✓
- `GotoLineState` in ui.rs ✓
- `FindReplaceState` in ui.rs ✓
- `RectangleSelectionState` in editor.rs ✓
- `Document.is_modified` in document.rs ✓
- `LayoutMsg::OpenFileInNewTab` in messages.rs ✓
- `Command::SelectNextOccurrence` and `Command::UnselectOccurrence` in command.rs ✓
- Config paths (`~/.config/token-editor/`) ✓

#### 📝 No Issues Found (Planned Features)

The following docs propose new code structures that don't claim to exist yet - these are correctly marked as planned:
- folding.md, soft-wrap.md, column-selection.md, whitespace-rendering.md, whitespace-management.md, auto-save.md, session-restore.md, recent-files.md, snippets.md, quick-open.md, markdown-preview.md, replace-enhancements.md, find-enhancements.md, goto-line-enhancements.md, command-palette-enhancements.md

---
